     8	
     9	## 1. What this is
    10	
    11	A database-backed personal planning system replacing a markdown-file system. It manages sprints, sprint items, tickets, and planning days for a single user (Khushal), and lets AI agents take tickets through a shaping-and-execution pipeline under explicit per-ticket permission ceilings. It runs entirely on the local machine.
    12	
    13	The system is: one SQLite database; one Python server process (JSON API + web UI + WebSocket event feed + embedded dispatcher + boundary scheduler); one CLI used by agents (the human operates through the UI, never the CLI); a set of skill documents as artifacts. Agents never mutate canonical state directly — they write proposals; code resolves them.
    14	
    15	## 2. Stack and runtime
    16	
    17	- Python ≥ 3.12, virtualenv at `.venv`, dependencies pinned in `requirements.txt`.
    18	- FastAPI + uvicorn for the server. SQLite (stdlib `sqlite3`) in WAL mode for storage. No ORM.
    19	- Frontend: no-build vanilla JS + CSS served as static files by the server. No bundler, no framework, no client-side state store.
    20	- Tests: pytest for unit; Playwright (Python, chromium, headless) for end-to-end. Lint: ruff. Typecheck: mypy (strict on `src/`).
    21	- The server binds `127.0.0.1` only. No auth in v1. Default port 8767 (configurable).
    22	- Layout: `src/planner/` organised by semantic domain — `core/` (shared infrastructure: db, events, config, server shell, adapters' interfaces) plus `tickets/`, `sprints/`, `days/`, `dispatch/`, `seed/`, `chat/`. Within every domain, organise by kind of work (contracts, logic, data, api) — layers within domains, never feature-slices, never one flat pile. Non-trivial things get folders.
    23	- Every tunable (timings, TTLs, limits, ordering weights, the boundary hour) lives in the configuration module (Section 13), never inline.
    24	- Repo root also carries: `assets/` (JS/CSS), `tests/unit/`, `tests/e2e/`, `tests/fixtures/`, `skills/`, `scripts/`, `data/` (gitignored; default DB location `data/planning.db`).
    25	- PRINCIPLES.md at the repo root binds this build; where this spec is explicit, the spec overrides. One explicit override: the core/module registry rule is out of scope for v1 — the planner is a single product with no pluggable modules; the registry pattern applies to adapters only (spawn, boundary/replan, gateway).
    26	
    27	## 3. Data model
    28	
    29	All entities carry `id` (short random slug with type prefix: `sp_`, `si_`, `t_`, `day_` (day ids are `day_YYYY-MM-DD`), `idea_`), `created_at`, `updated_at` (unix seconds). All state changes append a row to an `events` table (`id` AUTOINCREMENT, `entity_id`, `kind`, `payload` JSON, `created_at`). Events are append-only: the code exposes no update or delete path for events.
    30	
    31	### 3.1 Sprint
    32	- `date_start`, `date_end` (inclusive, ISO dates), `name`.
    33	- Kickoff fields (markdown text): `limiting_factor`, `primary_bet`, `supports`, `premortem`. Frozen after `kickoff_frozen_at` is set — writes rejected except `weekly_addenda` (append-only list of `{date, text}`).
    34	- Review fields (markdown text): `outcomes`, `solo_reflection`, `joint_discussion`, `updates_to_thinking`, `carry_forward`. Frozen after `review_frozen_at` is set.
    35	- The "current" sprint is the one whose date range contains the current planning date. Ranges must not overlap; creation rejects overlap.
    36	
    37	### 3.2 Sprint item
    38	- Fields: `title`, `body` (markdown), `status` ∈ {`todo`, `active`, `done`, `blocked`, `deferred_next_sprint`}, `priority` ∈ {P0, P1, P2, P3}, `deadline` (nullable ISO date), `project` ∈ {`Vylo`, `Tribe`, `Learning`, `Other`}, `current_state_note` (markdown), `sprint_id` (nullable).
    39	- `sprint_id = NULL` means backlog/deferred work. Assigning/unassigning a sprint is a plain field update (event-logged), never a copy.
    40	- `blocked` status carries `blocked_by`: a non-empty list of ticket ids. When every blocker reaches ticket-state `done`, the item is flagged `blockers_cleared = true` (derived, not a status change).
    41	- Agent-permitted direct transitions: `todo ↔ active`, and setting `blocked` with blocker links. `done` and `deferred_next_sprint` require a proposal accepted by the human (Section 4.4 mechanics apply, with the item's single gating field being `status`).
    42	
    43	### 3.3 Ticket
    44	- Fields: `title`, `state` (Section 4.1), `priority` ∈ {P0..P3} (default P3), `deadline` (nullable date), `project` (same enum, nullable when parented — derived from parent's item), `sprint_item_id` (nullable), `sprint_id` (nullable; writable only when `sprint_item_id` is NULL — writes rejected otherwise; when parented it is derived from the parent item), `recap` (markdown, writable only when state is past `needs_success`; writes before that are rejected with a structured error), `ceiling` (a ticket state; Section 4.3), `at_cap` ∈ {`stop`, `propose`}, `auto_blocked` (bool, Section 7.5), `chat_session_key` (nullable string), `fields` (Section 4.2).
    45	- Title length ≤ 200 chars, enforced on every write path.
    46	
    47	### 3.4 Day
    48	- `id = day_YYYY-MM-DD` where the date is the **planning date**: local time shifted by the boundary hour (Section 6.1). Day rows are created on first read or write for a date; reading a nonexistent day materializes it empty. There is no "missing day" state.
    49	- Fields: `brief` (markdown), `notes` (markdown), `plan` (Section 6.3 tree, JSON), plus an ordered ticket list: `day_tickets(day_id, ticket_id, position)` with contiguous positions from 0. Removing a ticket from a day deletes the association only (this is "deferring"; ticket state is untouched) and logs a `day_ticket_removed` event.
    50	
    51	### 3.5 Idea
    52	- `title`, `body` (markdown), `project` (nullable, same enum). No priority, no status, no other fields.
    53	
    54	### 3.6 Links
    55	- One table: `links(from_id, to_id, kind)` with kind ∈ {`belongs_to` (ticket→sprint item), `parent_child` (ticket→ticket), `blocks` (ticket→ticket or ticket→sprint item), `relates` (any→any)}.
    56	- A ticket has at most one `belongs_to` link (enforced). Self-links rejected. `blocks` and `parent_child` cycles rejected at write time (transitive check).
    57	- A ticket is **blocked** iff it is the target of a `blocks` link whose source ticket is not `done` or `dropped`. Blocked tickets are ineligible for dispatch (Section 7.2).
    58	
    59	## 4. Ticket lifecycle and the write model
    60	
    61	### 4.1 States
    62	
    63	`needs_success → needs_approach → needs_plan → in_progress → needs_review → done`, plus `dropped` (terminal, reachable from any non-`done` state, human-only action).
    64	
    65	There is no stored "ready" or "satisfied" state. Derived views (Section 4.5) express them.
    66	
    67	### 4.2 Fields as structured objects
    68	
    69	`tickets.fields` is a JSON object with exactly four keys: `success`, `approach`, `plan`, `result`. Each is `{value: string|null, proposal: {body, proposed_by, created_at}|null, notes: string|null}`. `value` is canonical and only ever written by the resolution engine (Section 4.4). `notes` is free-guidance text ("when doing the approach, consider X"), writable directly by human or agent at any time (event-logged, no state effect).
    70	
    71	Each pre-terminal state has one **gating field**:
    72	
    73	| state | gating field | accepted proposal advances to |
    74	|---|---|---|
    75	| needs_success | success | needs_approach |
    76	| needs_approach | approach | needs_plan |
    77	| needs_plan | plan | in_progress |
    78	| in_progress | result | needs_review (or done, Section 4.4) |
    79	| needs_review | — (human approval of `result.value`) | done |
    80	
    81	The `result.notes` slot is the ticket's **review notes**: the specific things that need careful checking, written during planning and updated as review happens. Review itself is not a new mechanism: in `needs_review`, an agent orchestrates the Codex CLI against the result and these notes, fixes what review surfaces, and updates the notes — then the human approves. No extra states, entities, or flow machinery for this.
    82	
    83	### 4.3 Ceiling and at-cap
    84	
    85	- The agent grant is two things, always together: **how far** (`ceiling`) and **what happens there** (`at_cap`). These are the whole vocabulary of agent permission.
    86	- `ceiling` is the highest state an agent-caused advancement may move the ticket **into**. Below the ceiling, agents work freely: proposals on the current gating field auto-accept and advance (Section 4.4). Human actions ignore the ceiling entirely (the human may jump a ticket to any state, including straight to `done`; skipped gating fields simply keep `value = null`).
    87	- `at_cap` governs the ticket once it sits at its ceiling: `stop` — agents may not file any proposal; the ticket rests untouched until the human changes the grant. `propose` — agents may file a proposal on the current gating field only; it parks for the human; nothing advances.
    88	- Defaults at creation: `ceiling = needs_success`, `at_cap = propose` (an agent may draft a success condition for approval; nothing advances) [RULING R2 default].
    89	
    90	### 4.4 The resolution engine
    91	
    92	The only write path for `fields.*.value` and for agent-caused state changes. Semantics, exactly:
    93	
    94	1. An agent files a proposal on a field (CLI/API). At most one pending proposal per field: a new proposal replaces the pending one (replacement logged as `proposal_superseded`).
    95	2. If the field is the current state's gating field AND the advance-target state ≤ `ceiling`: the proposal auto-accepts — `value` ← proposal body, proposal cleared, state advances one step, events `proposal_accepted` + `state_changed` logged with `resolved_by: "auto"`.
    96	3. Otherwise the proposal stays pending. Pending proposals on the current gating field put the ticket in the human approval queue.
    97	4. Human resolution of a pending proposal: **accept** (value ← body), **edit** (value ← human-edited text; logged as accepted with `edited: true`), or leave it and discuss in chat. There is no reject action; a proposal can be superseded or the ticket dropped.
    98	5. Special case `in_progress`: an accepted `result` advances to `needs_review` unless `ceiling = done`, in which case it advances directly to `done`. Human approval in `needs_review` (a dedicated approve action, not a field proposal) sets `done`.
    99	6. Every transition writes exactly one `state_changed` event `{from, to, cause}`. One canonical function per transition edge in the code; no second writer.
   100	7. **The onward grant.** Human acceptance of a gating-field proposal (accept or edit-accept) requires the explicit grant pair: `next_ceiling` — any state at or beyond the newly entered state, or `none` meaning the ceiling becomes exactly the newly entered state — AND `at_cap` (`stop`: agents don't even propose from here; `propose`: agents may draft the next gating field for approval). Both are stated on every accept, even when the answer is "nothing further"; an accept without the pair is rejected with a structured error. Agent auto-accepts change neither ceiling nor at_cap. The `needs_review` approve action and sprint-item status accepts carry no onward grant (they are terminal for agent involvement).
   101	
   102	### 4.5 Derived views (must exist as queryable API endpoints, not stored columns)
   103	
   104	- **Approval queue**: tickets with a pending proposal on their current gating field (plus sprint items with pending status proposals, plus `needs_review` tickets). Ordered oldest-pending first.
   105	- **Pickup queue** (dispatcher eligibility): Section 7.2.
   106	- **Overdue**: tickets/items with `deadline` < current planning date and not `done`/`dropped`.
   107	
   108	## 5. Sprints — behavior rules
   109	
   110	- Creating a sprint with kickoff fields sets nothing frozen; an explicit `freeze-kickoff` action sets `kickoff_frozen_at`. Same pattern for review. Frozen-field writes are rejected with a structured error.
   111	- Sprint view data: items grouped by status, each with its ticket rollup (counts by ticket state), plus a **loose tickets** section: tickets whose `sprint_id` = this sprint and `sprint_item_id` IS NULL.
   112	
   113	## 6. Days, the boundary, and the day plan
   114	
   115	### 6.1 Planning date
   116	
   117	Boundary hour: **05:00 local** [RULING R1 default]. Planning date = calendar date of (now − 5 hours). At 04:59 local on July 5 the planning date is July 4; at 05:00 it is July 5. Every "today" in the system uses this rule. In test mode (Section 13) the clock is injectable.
   118	
   119	### 6.2 The boundary job
   120	
   121	Runs once per planning date, at the first scheduler tick at/after the boundary hour (and never twice for the same date; a `boundary_runs` table records completions). It has a deterministic pass (pure code) and a judgment pass (agent adapter):
   122	
   123	Deterministic pass:
   124	1. Materialize the new day row.
   125	2. Compute carryover candidates: yesterday's day-tickets whose ticket state is not `done`/`dropped`.
   126	3. Compute overdue list and the approval-queue digest.
   127	4. Close out yesterday: log a `day_closed` event with a summary payload {counts of done/not-done day tickets}.
   128	
   129	Judgment pass (via the **boundary agent adapter**, one call): given the deterministic outputs, it returns `{brief_markdown, plan_tree}`. The result is stored as the day's `brief` and a **proposed** plan (Section 6.3). If the adapter fails or times out (60s), the day still exists with an empty brief and no plan; the failure is event-logged. v1 evidence is DB-internal only — no repo or session scanning [RULING R4 default].
   130	
   131	If the human has already planned the day explicitly (any day-ticket or accepted plan exists) before the boundary job's judgment pass, the judgment pass is skipped entirely.
   132	
   133	### 6.3 The day-plan tree
   134	
   135	`day.plan` is JSON: `{root: {focus: string, status}, children: [{ticket_id, note, status, position}]}` — one level of children in v1. Node `status` ∈ {`proposed`, `accepted`, `invalidated`}.
   136	
   137	- Accepting a node sets it `accepted`. **Accept-all** accepts root and all children; accepting the root alone does not cascade. When a child with a `ticket_id` is accepted, the ticket is added to the day's ticket list (at the end) if not already present.
   138	- Invalidating the root sets root and every child `invalidated` and triggers one replan-adapter call that returns a complete new tree (replacing the old; old tree preserved in the event payload). Invalidating a child replans only that node: the adapter returns a replacement child. Replans serialize; if an invalidation arrives while a replan is in flight, the in-flight result is discarded and one new replan runs (latest wins) [RULING R5 default].
   139	- **Reject-all**: clears `plan` to null (event-logged); the human plans manually.
   140	
   141	## 7. The dispatcher and runs
   142	
   143	### 7.1 Tick
   144	
   145	Every 60s (configurable), inside the server process. Order within a tick: (1) reclaim expired claims and detect dead worker processes; (2) recompute eligibility; (3) spawn up to the concurrency cap. A machine-wide advisory file lock (`data/dispatcher.lock`) guarantees a single dispatcher; a `dispatch_enabled` config flag is re-read every tick and fails safe to `false` on config read error.
   146	
   147	### 7.2 Eligibility and ordering
   148	
   149	A ticket is eligible iff ALL of: not `done`/`dropped`/`needs_review`; not blocked (3.6); not `auto_blocked`; no active claim; and an agent can do work on it — meaning the current gating field has no pending proposal AND either (a) the advance-target ≤ `ceiling` (the agent can move it), or (b) the ticket is at its ceiling with `at_cap = propose` (the agent can draft the gating field for approval). A ticket at its ceiling with `at_cap = stop` is never eligible. Ordering: priority (P0 first), then deadline ascending with NULLs last, then `created_at` ascending.
   150	
   151	### 7.3 Claims and runs
   152	
   153	- Claim = CAS: `UPDATE tickets SET claim_lock=?, claim_expires=? WHERE id=? AND claim_lock IS NULL`; rowcount 0 = lost. TTL 15 minutes. Every claim inserts a `runs` row (`ticket_id`, `status` ∈ {running, done, blocked, crashed, timed_out, reclaimed}, `started_at`, `ended_at`, `summary`, `error`, `pid`).
   154	- Heartbeat (CLI verb) extends `claim_expires` by one TTL. Expired claim or dead PID → run closed `reclaimed`, lock cleared.
   155	- Max concurrent runs: 2 [RULING R7 default]. Per-run max runtime: 30 minutes → SIGTERM, run `timed_out`.
   156	
   157	### 7.4 Spawning
   158	
   159	Through a **spawn adapter** (subprocess boundary, mockable): `hermes -p <profile> --skills <skill> chat -q "work planning ticket <id>"`, detached session, per-run log file under `data/logs/`, env: `PLAN_SERVER_URL`, `PLAN_TICKET_ID`, `PLAN_RUN_ID`, `PLAN_CLAIM`. Profile and skill names come from config [RULING R3 default: profile `default`, skill `planning-worker`]. Tests never spawn real processes.
   160	
   161	### 7.5 Circuit breaker
   162	
   163	A run ending `crashed`, `timed_out`, or `spawn_failed` increments `tickets.consecutive_failures`; a run ending `done` resets it to 0. Reaching 2 sets `auto_blocked = true` (sticky; event `auto_blocked`). The dispatcher never picks up an auto-blocked ticket; a human clear-action resets both flag and counter.
   164	
   165	### 7.6 Server-side write validation
   166	
   167	Every agent write (proposal, recap, heartbeat, run close) must carry `PLAN_RUN_ID` + `PLAN_CLAIM` matching the ticket's active claim, except proposal/recap writes from non-dispatched agent contexts (no claim env — e.g. the planner-main chat agent), which are permitted with the proposer taken from `PLAN_ACTOR` (default `agent`). Human writes happen through the UI/API only — the CLI is an agent tool. A write with a stale/foreign claim is rejected with a structured error naming the mismatch.
   168	
   169	## 8. The CLI (`plan`)
   170	
   171	A single entry point (`plan`, installed via `pip install -e .` console script) speaking HTTP to the server. **The CLI is for agents (and developer debugging); the human never uses it — every human action lives in the UI.** No resolution verbs exist in it: accepting, approving, granting, unblocking, and day-plan decisions are UI/API-only. Global behavior: `--json` on every verb (machine output; stable exit codes: 0 success, 1 validation/domain error, 2 connection error); long text via `--body-file <path>` or `-` for stdin — body text is never passed as an inline argument; env defaults `PLAN_SERVER_URL`, `PLAN_TICKET_ID` (verbs taking a ticket id use it when omitted), `PLAN_RUN_ID`, `PLAN_CLAIM`, `PLAN_ACTOR`.
   172	
   173	Verbs (exact):
   174	- `plan serve` — run the server (foreground).
   175	- `plan seed --source <dir>` / `plan seed --demo` — Section 12.
   176	- `plan ticket create|show|list|set` (`set` for priority/deadline/day-assignment/sprint-assignment only; ceiling and at_cap are not CLI-settable — grants are human, in the UI).
   177	- `plan propose <field>` (body via stdin/file) · `plan recap` (same input rules) · `plan note <field>`.
   178	- `plan item create|show|list|set|propose-status` (`set` limited to the agent-permitted transitions of Section 3.2) · `plan sprint show` · `plan idea create|list`.
   179	- `plan day show [date]|add-ticket|remove-ticket`.
   180	- `plan link add|rm <from> <to> --kind <kind>` · `plan run heartbeat|close --outcome <o> --summary -`.
   181	- `plan queue approvals|pickup|overdue` — the derived views (read-only).
   182	
   183	## 9. HTTP API and events
   184	
   185	- REST JSON under `/api/`: CRUD + actions mirroring Section 8 verbs, PLUS the human actions that deliberately have no CLI form — accept/edit-accept with the grant pair, `needs_review` approve, unblock, grant changes, sprint create/freeze, day-plan accept/accept-all/invalidate/reject-all, drop — and `GET /api/board`, `GET /api/day/<date>`, `GET /api/sprint/current`, `GET /api/queues`. Every mutating endpoint routes through the same canonical functions (one writer per edge).
   186	- `WS /api/events?since=<id>`: tails the `events` table (poll interval 300ms server-side), pushing `{events:[...], cursor}` batches. Used by the UI as an **invalidation signal only** — the UI refetches JSON endpoints (debounced 250ms); it never reconciles event payloads into local state.
   187	- Test mode (Section 13): `POST /api/test/tick-boundary` and `POST /api/test/tick-dispatcher` run one tick synchronously; available only when test mode is on, else 404.
   188	
   189	## 10. The UI
   190	
   191	Six screens, served at `/`. Simplicity is the ruling aesthetic: fewer things done cleanly. Every screen renders from server JSON; refresh at any moment restores identical state.
   192	
   193	Design system (per PRINCIPLES.md, binding):
   194	- One token file, `assets/tokens.css`, owns everything themable: surfaces, text tokens, accent (bright/surface/text), radius scale, motion durations (fast/base/slow), spacing scale, border widths. The app's entire personality must be tunable by editing this one file. No new token categories without evidence of need.
   195	- Type: strict scale, five sizes maximum. Motion: entrances ease out, nothing bounces, all durations from motion tokens. Add nothing to a screen unless it makes the user feel something or a smart person genuinely needs it to understand the screen; no explanatory text for the obvious.
   196	- Before writing any component, record the component inventory for all five screens in decisions.md; build few, reuse hard, no near-duplicates.
   197	
   198	1. **Day (home)**: the brief (rendered markdown); the day-plan tree with per-node Accept / Invalidate and top-level Accept-all / Reject-all; the day's ticket list (ordered); a Review entry showing the pending count; and the day chat panel (Section 11) — quick capture happens here, by telling the planner, which files tickets/ideas via the CLI (Section 17).
   199	2. **Review**: the one-at-a-time review surface — one home of approvals (the Ticket screen is the other), and where the morning pass happens. Pending items — gating-field proposals, sprint-item status proposals, and `needs_review` results — presented one at a time, oldest first. Each shows the proposal rendered, a quick-edit textarea, Accept (with the mandatory grant-pair picker of Section 4.4: how far, and stop-vs-propose — plain pickers, including "no further"), Skip (item stays queued, move on), and a link to the full Ticket. Resolved items leave the queue immediately.
   200	3. **Board**: one column per ticket state (`dropped` hidden), cards show title, priority, deadline, project, pending-proposal marker, running-claim marker. No drag-and-drop in v1: card click opens Ticket.
   201	4. **Ticket**: all four fields as sections (value rendered as markdown; pending proposal shown with Accept/Edit, accepts carrying the onward grant; notes editable inline), recap, state control, ceiling and at-cap as plain pickers (no dial widgetry), a **Copy ticket** button (copies a plain-text block — title, state, priority, field values, recap, links — to the clipboard for pasting into any agent or editor), links, day/sprint assignment, run history, event log, and the chat panel (Section 11).
   202	5. **Sprint**: kickoff fields, items grouped by status (with ticket rollup counts and blockers-cleared flags), loose tickets, review fields (frozen states shown as locked).
   203	6. **Backlog & Ideas**: itemless sprint items list (by priority) and ideas list, each with create forms.
   204	
   205	## 11. Chat
   206	
   207	Each ticket and each day has at most one chat session. The chat panel connects through a **gateway adapter** wrapping the Hermes gateway (`tui_gateway.ws` import, same pattern as the old viewer). If the gateway is unreachable the panel renders "gateway offline" and the rest of the UI is unaffected. The entity's `chat_session_key` persists the session id. In tests the adapter is a fake that echoes. Live gateway behavior is a post-run human item, not an acceptance test.
   208	
   209	## 12. Seed and migration
   210	
   211	Migration from the markdown system is an in-run deliverable, not an afterthought. A frozen snapshot of the real planning data (taken 2026-07-04) is committed at `migration/source-snapshot/`; the build must migrate it successfully. The live directory is still never read at build/test time — the snapshot is the real-data target; final live cutover at switch time is the human's moment (§18.4).
   212	
   213	`plan seed --source <dir>` reads a markdown planning directory with the current system's shapes and imports: `sprints/current/sprint-kickoff.md` → sprint kickoff fields; `sprint-tracking.md` items → sprint items (statuses: Todo→todo, In Progress→active, Done→done, Blocked→blocked, Deferred→deferred_next_sprint; `Priority:`/`Urgency:`/`Project:` fields mapped; Mode dropped); `sprint-review.md` → review fields; the latest `daily/YYYY-MM-DD/workspace.md` tickets → tickets (Readiness mapping: Concepts→needs_success, Needs Shaping→needs_approach, Ready→needs_plan, In Progress→in_progress; `Ticket ID:` preserved as an alias field; `Chat ID:` → `chat_session_key`; `Priority:` mapped; body/success/approach text into the matching fields' values) linked to sprint items by title match when unambiguous, else standalone with sprint assignment; `deferred.md` → sprint items with `sprint_id = NULL` (project sections and P-labels mapped); `ideas.md` → ideas. `plan seed --demo` creates a deterministic demo dataset for dogfooding and manual testing: 8 tickets spanning every state with varied priorities, deadlines, and ceilings; one blocking link; one parent sprint item with children; one planned day. It requires an empty database and errors otherwise.
   214	
   215	Archives and historical daily folders are not imported — only the latest day's workspace feeds tickets [RULING R6 default]. Seeding is idempotent by alias/title: re-running against the same source creates no duplicates. Every seed run emits a migration report (printed, and with `--json` structured): counts imported per entity kind, plus an explicit list of skipped/unparseable sections — silent drops are forbidden. Unit-tested against synthetic fixtures in `tests/fixtures/`; additionally proven end-to-end against `migration/source-snapshot/` (item 34), whose known ground truth is: 1 sprint (2026-07-01 → 2026-07-12); 12 sprint items (6 todo, 5 active, 1 done — the done item titled "Ship waitlist mechanics."); 4 tickets from the 2026-07-03 workspace (mic-publish and app-typography at needs_plan; landing-gate-1 and durable-personas at in_progress; `Chat ID: 20260702_114500_0ec57a` preserved on landing-gate-1); 9 deferred items (NULL sprint, projects mapped from the Vylo/Tribe/Learning/Other headings); 20 ideas. The live directory is never read in tests.
   216	
   217	## 13. Configuration and test mode
   218	
   219	- `config.yaml` at repo root (checked in with defaults) + env overrides: `PLAN_DB_PATH` (default `data/planning.db`), `PLAN_PORT` (8767), `PLAN_BOUNDARY_HOUR` (5), `PLAN_TICK_SECONDS` (60), `PLAN_CLAIM_TTL_SECONDS` (900), `PLAN_MAX_RUNS` (2), `PLAN_FAILURE_LIMIT` (2), `PLAN_DISPATCH_ENABLED` (true), `PLAN_HERMES_BIN`, `PLAN_HERMES_PROFILE`, `PLAN_WORKER_SKILL`.
   220	- Test mode: `PLAN_TEST_MODE=1` enables `PLAN_FAKE_NOW` (ISO datetime honored everywhere the clock is read) and the `/api/test/*` endpoints. When `PLAN_TEST_MODE` is unset, `PLAN_FAKE_NOW` is ignored and test endpoints 404.
   221	
   222	## 14. Structural rules
   223	
   224	- **One canonical writer per transition edge.** Every state/status change goes through exactly one function; API and CLI both call it.
   225	- **Proposals-only for agents.** No code path lets a request carrying claim env write `fields.*.value` or change state except via the resolution engine.
   226	- **Adapters at every external boundary**: spawn (subprocess), boundary/replan agent, gateway chat. Each is a small interface with a real and a fake implementation; tests use fakes; nothing in tests shells out to `hermes` or opens network connections beyond localhost Playwright↔server.
   227	- **Append-only events**; derived views computed on read, never stored.
   228	- **Contracts first**: each domain has a contracts file (types, enums, shapes) generated at stage 1; all implementation imports its types from contracts and never redeclares a shape locally; the frontend consumes exactly the JSON shapes the contracts document. A shape change means changing the contract file and letting type errors drive the fixes.
   229	- **Pure logic dependency-free**: state machine, resolution, eligibility, ordering, seed parsing, and planning-date math live in each domain's logic layer as plain functions importing nothing but stdlib (and domain contracts); FastAPI/DB code imports them, never the reverse. The acid test: if a rule can't be unit-tested with no mocks, it's in the wrong place.
   230	- **Audio capture seam (future, reserved).** Audio capture is not in v1, but its arrival point is fixed now: it will be its own isolated domain module behind an adapter interface, feeding the chat input. Build the chat input as a component that accepts pluggable input sources, and couple nothing to the assumption that input is typed text — dropping the audio module in later must touch its own new folder and one registration, nothing else.
   231	- Never touch `~/.hermes/planning/` (the live markdown system), `~/.hermes/hermes-agent/`, or any path outside this repository at build/test time.
   232	
   233	## 15. Non-goals (v1)
   234	
   235	No push notifications, no iOS client, no auth/multi-user, no weekly-check cron, no repo/session evidence adapters, no Necessary-Calls entity, no Mode field, no drag-and-drop, no markdown export mirror, no archive import, no diff-style proposal views, no staleness/deadline-horizon/sprint-health widgets, no instructional empty states, no mobile-specific layout, no command palette, no keyboard-shortcut layer, no activity/away digest, no audio capture (its seam is reserved in Section 14).
   236	
   237	## 16. Rulings in force
   238	
   239	Defaults the owner may override before the run; each is tagged where it binds: **R1** boundary hour 05:00. **R2** new-ticket grant `ceiling=needs_success, at_cap=propose`. **R3** spawn profile `default`, worker skill `planning-worker`. **R4** boundary evidence DB-internal only. **R5** replan serialization, latest-wins. **R6** seed excludes archives. **R7** max concurrent runs 2.
   240	
   241	## 17. Skills artifacts
   242	
   243	Write four skill documents under `skills/` (not installed anywhere by the build): `planning-worker.md` (how a dispatched agent works a ticket via the CLI: orient with `plan ticket show --json`, propose via stdin, recap discipline, heartbeats, never asking questions), `planning-boundary.md` (the judgment pass contract), `planner-main.md` (the day/system chat agent: operating the queues and day plan via CLI, and quick capture — anything the human tosses into the day chat that is work or an idea gets filed as a standalone ticket or idea via the CLI, immediately, defaulting to P3), `planning-executor.md` (working an in_progress ticket you own, including the review discipline: at needs_review, orchestrate the Codex CLI against the result and the review notes, fix what it surfaces, update the notes). Each ≤ 150 lines, written for the agent that will load it. DOCS.md summarizes their roles.
   244	
   245	---
   246	
   247	## 18. Delivery expectations and acceptance checklist
   248	
   249	**Build in this order**: (1) contracts skeleton — schema DDL, enums, typed models, adapter interfaces, config loading, CLI/API surface stubs; (2) **the verify instrument** (18.2), runnable with every checklist item FAIL; (3) pure-logic modules + unit tests (items 1–21 and 36) green; (4) server wiring — API, WS, resolution engine over DB, dispatcher, boundary scheduler, CLI against the API; (5) UI views; (6) Playwright e2e suite, items 22–34 green; (7) the fake-ticket dogfood pass (Section 18.5): Level A green (item 35), Levels B and C executed with evidence recorded in DOGFOOD.md. Do not start a later stage while an earlier stage's tests fail.
   250	
   251	Where this document delegates a choice, make it, implement it fully, and record it in decisions.md. If a genuine contradiction emerges, state it in PROGRESS.md and propose a resolution consistent with Section 14's rules — do not silently pick.
   252	
   253	### 18.1 Required artifacts
   254	
   255	- **PROGRESS.md** — updated every work cycle: current stage, what just passed, current hypothesis, next step, blockers. First read after any context compaction. Three failed attempts on one problem → log it and change approach materially.
   256	- **decisions.md** — every delegated choice, briefly justified.
   257	- **DOCS.md** — plain-language documentation written progressively as each stage completes, never retrofitted. A smart non-engineer must be able to read it: what the planner is, the entities, how proposals and ceilings work, what the dispatcher does, how a day flows, what each screen shows, what the CLI verbs do, what the skills are for. If a section needs the code to be understood, rewrite the section.
   258	- **DOGFOOD.md** — the record of the Section 18.5 fake-ticket dogfood: per level, what was run, the exact commands, the evidence (event-log excerpts, run rows, session/log file paths under `data/logs/`), and an honest outcome. Narrative without corresponding logs is not evidence.
   259	
   260	### 18.2 The verify instrument
   261	
   262	- One command: `./verify` (executable script at repo root). Runs in order: ruff, mypy, unit suite, build check (`python -m compileall src/` + `node --check` on every file in `assets/`), then the Playwright e2e suite headless. Prints a per-item scoreboard for every acceptance test below (item number, name, PASS/FAIL) and ends with exactly `VERIFY: N/36 PASS`.
   263	- The instrument fails with an explicit message if any test file contains `@pytest.mark.skip`, `pytest.skip(`, `xfail`, `.only`, a commented-out test, or an empty test body — the scan itself is unit-tested (item 21).
   264	- Built at stage 2, before implementation; all items report FAIL until their stage lands. The scoreboard is the single source of truth for completeness.
   265	- Unit items are 1–21 and 36; end-to-end items are 22–35. Item 34 (snapshot migration) runs against `migration/source-snapshot/`, and item 35 (dogfood Level A) is the scripted walkthrough — both part of the suite, not manual steps. Levels B and C of the dogfood (Section 18.5) are deliberately OUTSIDE `./verify`: they use live agents and belong to stage 7's evidence, not the deterministic gate.
   266	
   267	### 18.3 Acceptance tests
   268	
   269	**Fences:**
   270	- Each item maps 1:1 to a named test whose name includes the item number (`test_a01_...`, `test_e22_...`).
   271	- Assertions use the specific values in this document — exact states, exact orderings, exact error shapes — not weakened approximations.
   272	- After first written, any change to a test requires a logged justification in decisions.md.
   273	- A claim of passing is valid only if `./verify` ran fresh in the same turn with full output shown.
   274	
   275	External boundaries (hermes spawn, boundary/replan agent, chat gateway) are faked in all tests. E2E uses a real server + temp SQLite DB + `PLAN_TEST_MODE=1`, driving ticks via the test endpoints and time via `PLAN_FAKE_NOW`. Multi-surface behavior uses two browser contexts.
   276	
   277	**Unit (pytest, pure logic + engine over a temp DB):**
   278	1. Planning-date math: 2026-07-05T04:59 local → planning date 2026-07-04; T05:00 → 2026-07-05; boundary hour honored from config.
   279	2. Gating chain: accepting success/approach/plan/result proposals advances exactly one state each, in order, with one `state_changed` event per step.
   280	3. Ceiling auto-accept: ceiling `needs_plan` with `at_cap = propose`, proposals filed on success then approach auto-accept and advance; the plan proposal stays pending (state `needs_plan`, ticket in approval queue).
   281	4. At-cap stop vs propose: at its ceiling with `at_cap = stop`, any agent proposal — including the current gating field — is rejected with the structured error; with `at_cap = propose`, a proposal on the current gating field files as pending and nothing advances; below the ceiling the behavior is identical for both values.
   282	5. One pending proposal per field: a second proposal supersedes the first; `proposal_superseded` event carries the replaced body.
   283	6. Edit-accept: accepting with edited text stores the edited text exactly as `value`, flags `edited: true` in the event.
   284	7. Result routing: accepted result with ceiling `needs_review` → state `needs_review`; with ceiling `done` → `done`; approve action in `needs_review` → `done`.
   285	8. Recap: write at `needs_success` rejected with structured error; write at `needs_approach` succeeds, overwrites prior recap, logs event, changes no state.
   286	9. Blocking: ticket blocked by an open ticket is dispatch-ineligible; blocker → `done` makes it eligible; `blocks` cycle creation rejected.
   287	10. Sprint-item permissions: agent `todo→active` succeeds; agent direct `active→done` write rejected; `done` via proposal + accept succeeds; `blocked_by` list stored and `blockers_cleared` computed when all blockers done.
   288	11. Dispatch ordering: fixture of five eligible tickets orders P0 before P1; equal priority by earlier deadline; NULL deadline last; then `created_at`.
   289	12. Day-ticket removal: deletes association only, position list re-packs contiguously, ticket state unchanged, `day_ticket_removed` logged.
   290	13. Sprint assignment rules: standalone ticket accepts `sprint_id`; parented ticket's `sprint_id` write rejected; parent's sprint derived.
   291	14. Claim CAS: two concurrent claim attempts on one ticket → exactly one wins, one `runs` row created.
   292	15. TTL/reclaim: expired claim reclaimed (run `reclaimed`, lock cleared, eligible again); heartbeat extends expiry by one TTL.
   293	16. Circuit breaker: two consecutive `crashed` runs → `auto_blocked`, ineligible; unblock action clears flag and counter; a `done` run resets the counter.
   294	17. Day-plan tree: root invalidation marks root + all children invalidated and requests one replan; child invalidation replaces only that child; accept-all accepts every node and adds child tickets to the day list exactly once.
   295	18. Boundary job (fake clock): first tick ≥ 05:00 creates the day, computes carryover (yesterday's non-done day tickets), overdue list, logs `day_closed`; second tick same date does nothing; explicit prior planning skips the judgment pass.
   296	19. Seed fixtures: importing `tests/fixtures/planning-md/` yields the exact expected entity counts and spot-checked values (item statuses, ticket states per the Readiness mapping, preserved Chat ID, deferred items with NULL sprint, idea with project); re-running seeds zero duplicates.
   297	20. Freeze rules: kickoff write after freeze rejected; `weekly_addenda` append still allowed; review same pattern. Sprint overlap rejected.
   298	21. Instrument integrity: the skip-scan detects each forbidden pattern (`@pytest.mark.skip`, `pytest.skip(`, `xfail`, empty body) in a synthetic file set and passes a clean set; `PLAN_FAKE_NOW` is ignored when `PLAN_TEST_MODE` is unset.
   299	
   300	**End-to-end (Playwright, chromium, two browser contexts where stated):**
   301	22. CLI create → live board: `plan ticket create` via subprocess with env pointing at the test server; the ticket appears on the Board in `needs_success` in a second context without reload.
   302	23. Env-pinned propose: `plan propose success` with `PLAN_TICKET_ID` set and a multi-line markdown body on stdin; Ticket screen shows the proposal rendered, intact.
   303	24. Accept in Review: the pending proposal appears on the Review screen; Accept is impossible without choosing the grant pair; choosing ("no further", stop) advances the ticket one state with ceiling equal to the new state and `at_cap = stop` (asserted via API); the item leaves the queue and both contexts update without reload.
   304	25. Edit-accept in Review: the quick-edit textarea comes prefilled with the proposal; submitting altered text with grant (`needs_plan`, propose) stores the altered text exactly as the field value on Ticket, sets the ceiling to `needs_plan` and `at_cap` to propose.
   305	26. Chat panel: opens on Ticket with the fake gateway (echo); a sent message renders a reply; `chat_session_key` persisted (asserted via API); gateway-offline fake renders the offline notice.
   306	27. Auto-accept chain e2e: ticket with ceiling `needs_plan` and `at_cap = propose`: two CLI proposals advance it to `needs_plan` with the plan proposal pending in the approval queue.
   307	28. Day view: with a fake boundary adapter, driving `POST /api/test/tick-boundary` at fake-now 05:01 produces the brief and a proposed tree; Accept-all adds the child tickets to the day list.
   308	29. Invalidation: invalidating the root calls the fake replan adapter and renders the replacement tree; invalidating one child replaces only that child (other nodes keep status).
   309	30. Dispatcher e2e: eligible ticket + fake spawn adapter; `POST /api/test/tick-dispatcher` claims it (run visible on Ticket); the fake worker's CLI calls (propose result with claim env) park it in `needs_review`; it appears in the approval queue; approve → `done`.
   310	31. Refresh restores state: on Day, Board, and Ticket mid-flow (pending proposal, running claim), a reload renders identical content.
   311	32. Sprint view live: `plan item set --status active` via CLI reflects on the Sprint screen in both contexts without reload; loose ticket appears in the loose section.
   312	33. Seed e2e: `plan seed --source tests/fixtures/planning-md/` against the test server, then Board/Sprint/Backlog show the fixture's expected titles and counts.
   313	34. Snapshot migration e2e: `plan seed --source migration/source-snapshot/` imports the frozen real data with exactly the ground truth stated in Section 12 (1 sprint with those dates; 12 items split 6/5/1 with "Ship waitlist mechanics." done; the 4 named tickets in their mapped states with the Chat ID preserved; 9 deferred items; 20 ideas); the migration report lists zero silently-skipped sections (anything unparsed is enumerated in it); a second run imports zero duplicates.
   314	35. Dogfood Level A: `scripts/dogfood_cli.py` drives the full workflow against a test server seeded with `plan seed --demo` — agent actions through the real CLI, human actions (accepts with grant pairs, needs_review approve, day-plan decisions) through the HTTP API, since the CLI deliberately has no human verbs: create a ticket; propose and accept through every gate — every accept carrying its grant pair — including one edit-accept and one superseded proposal; write and overwrite the recap; assign to the day and remove; create a blocking link and confirm dispatch-ineligibility, then complete the blocker and confirm eligibility; claim via the test dispatcher tick; propose result with claim env; approve from needs_review to done. The script asserts each step's resulting state via `--json`/response output and exits non-zero on the first mismatch.
   315	36. Onward grant (unit): a human accept missing either half of the grant pair is rejected with the structured error and changes nothing; accept with (`none`, `stop`) leaves the ticket resting — a subsequent agent proposal is rejected; accept with (`none`, `propose`) permits a pending proposal on the new gating field; accept with a later ceiling permits advancement to it; agent auto-accepts change neither ceiling nor at_cap; the `needs_review` approve action requires no grant.
   316	
   317	### 18.5 Fake-ticket dogfood (stage 7)
   318	
   319	The application must be proven with realistic fake tickets at three escalating levels of automation. This is the one sanctioned exception to the fakes-only rule: Levels B and C use the real local Hermes runtime — never inside `./verify`, always recorded in DOGFOOD.md with log evidence.
   320	
   321	- **Level A — the CLI as a user and agent would drive it.** The scripted walkthrough of item 35. Deterministic, gating, part of `./verify`.
   322	- **Level B — a Hermes agent works a fake ticket.** Spawn one real session: `hermes chat -q "Read <repo>/skills/planning-worker.md, then work planning ticket <id> using the plan CLI"` with the agent env vars pinned (server URL + ticket id) against a running server with a demo-seeded ticket shaped for it (ceiling `needs_plan`, `at_cap = propose`). Pass evidence, mechanical: at least one proposal and one recap write recorded on that ticket by the session (asserted from the event log), session output captured under `data/logs/` and referenced in DOGFOOD.md. The repo's skill artifacts are exercised via the read-this-file prompt because they are not installed into `~/.hermes/skills/` during the run.
   323	- **Level C — a dispatched ticket agent end to end.** Enable the real spawn adapter (config), one eligible demo ticket, real dispatcher tick: claim → run row → the spawned agent writes at least one proposal carrying the claim env → run closes with a terminal outcome and the ticket lands where its ceiling dictates. Evidence: the run row, the event sequence, and the per-run log path, all in DOGFOOD.md.
   324	
   325	Levels B and C depend on live agent behavior: apply the three-attempt rule per level (materially different approach each time — prompt shape, ticket shaping, ceiling), and record every attempt honestly in DOGFOOD.md. Completion requires both levels' mechanical evidence. If server, CLI, or dispatcher code changes after a level's evidence was produced, that level is stale and must be re-run.
   326	
   327	### 18.4 Post-run human pass (not tests)
   328	
   329	Live Hermes gateway chat; live dispatcher spawning a real agent; the final cutover seed against the live `~/.hermes/planning/` at switch time (the in-run migration proves the machinery on the frozen snapshot; the live directory will have drifted since 2026-07-04 and its import is reviewed by the human); installing skills into `~/.hermes/skills/`; launchd supervision; UI look-and-feel judgment.

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# Engineering & Design Principles
     2	
     3	These are standing rules. They apply to any project unless the spec explicitly overrides them.
     4	
     5	## Codebase structure
     6	- Top level is organised by semantic domain (e.g. rooms/, sharing/, billing/, games/<game>/). Within every domain, organise by kind of work: logic/algorithms together, data layer together, components together, views together. Layers within domains — never feature-slices within domains, never one flat pile.
     7	- If something is non-trivial, it gets a folder. Folders are cheap and create structure that lets things expand or be isolated later.
     8	- Anything intended to be tuned later (sizes, formulas, timings, limits) lives in an isolated configuration module, not inline.
     9	
    10	## Core/module contract
    11	- Shared infrastructure (core) and features (modules) are separated by a defined contract. A module declares its configuration, states/phases, and views; core provides the shared services and renders from a registry.
    12	- Modules register through a single manifest/registry. Core contains zero module-specific conditionals — if core has an `if` on a module id, the structure is wrong.
    13	- The test: adding the next module means creating a new folder and one registration line. If it requires changing core, stop and fix the structure.
    14	
    15	## Contracts first
    16	- Before implementation: generate the skeleton — schema, enums, interfaces, registry shape, design tokens, and a contracts/types file per domain.
    17	- All implementation imports its types from contracts. Never redeclare a shape locally; never let the client invent its own version of a server type. If a contract must change, change the contract file and let type errors drive the fixes.
    18	
    19	## Pure logic
    20	- All business rules are dependency-free: plain functions in each domain's logic/ layer, zero imports from the backend framework, UI framework, or anything with side effects. Backend functions call rules; they do not contain them.
    21	- The acid test: if a rule can't be unit-tested with no mocks, it's in the wrong place.
    22	
    23	## External services
    24	- Every external service sits behind an adapter interface. No provider types, URLs, or response shapes exist anywhere outside its adapter. Swapping providers touches one new adapter file and its registration, nothing else.
    25	- Fetch once, own forever: when external data enters application state, persist the full record; downstream reads come from our store, never a re-fetch.
    26	- Do not build fallback providers, retry choreography, or speculative resilience unless the spec demands it. A failed call gets a calm retry state.
    27	
    28	## Design system
    29	- Everything themable lives in one token file: surfaces, text tokens, accent (bright/surface/text), radius scale, motion durations (fast/base/slow), spacing scale, border widths. The app's entire personality must be tunable by editing this one file.
    30	- New token categories require evidence of need. Do not invent them speculatively.
    31	- Minimal means selectively punchy, not timid: restrained surfaces, then deliberate moments of full impact. Add nothing to the UI unless it makes the user feel something or a smart person genuinely needs it to understand the screen. No explanatory text for the obvious.
    32	- Type: strict scale, five sizes maximum. Hierarchy and spacing rhythm create calm.
    33	- Motion: smooth and decisive, never slow. Entrances ease out. Nothing bounces. All durations come from motion tokens. Long animated traversals decompose into distinct beats rather than one heroic continuous move.
    34	- Prefer making the design system carry function over bolting on widgets (e.g. a border draining as a timer rather than a countdown number). Structural elements doing double duty beats added chrome.
    35	- Plan the component inventory for the whole flow before writing components. Build few, reuse hard. No near-duplicates.
    36	
    37	## Spec and test discipline
    38	- Specs are written in imperatives. Qualifiers ("perhaps", "some form of") are converted to decisions or explicitly delegated with criteria ("you choose X; it must satisfy Y"). Silent ambiguity is a bug in the spec.
    39	- Acceptance tests assert specific stated values, never vibes. Tests are part of the specification: they map 1:1 to requirements by name, and changing one after first writing requires a logged justification.
    40	- Verification means running the check fresh and showing full output. Results are never asserted from memory.

/bin/zsh -lc 'nl -ba assets/api.js' in /Users/khushaljagota/.hermes/planning-v2
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/* Data layer: JSON fetch wrapper (structured-error passthrough), the WS client,
     2	 * and the invalidation bus (SPEC §9). No client-side state store — the bus holds
     3	 * only a cursor, a debounce timer, a backoff delay, and one flush callback; every
     4	 * flush makes the active screen refetch. WS batches are an invalidation signal:
     5	 * onmessage reads only msg.cursor, never the events array.
     6	 * Classic script: attaches Planner.api and Planner.bus; defines window.__plannerDebug. */
     7	(function () {
     8	  "use strict";
     9	  var Planner = (window.Planner = window.Planner || {});
    10	  var config = Planner.config;
    11	
    12	  // The sanctioned console hook: counters only, one console.debug per flush / open.
    13	  window.__plannerDebug = { flushes: 0, wsOpens: 0, cursor: 0 };
    14	
    15	  function makeError(code, message, extra) {
    16	    var err = new Error(message);
    17	    err.code = code;
    18	    if (extra) {
    19	      Object.keys(extra).forEach(function (key) {
    20	        err[key] = extra[key];
    21	      });
    22	    }
    23	    return err;
    24	  }
    25	
    26	  function makePlannerError(envelope, status) {
    27	    var err = new Error(envelope.message);
    28	    err.code = envelope.code;
    29	    err.detail = envelope.detail;
    30	    err.status = status;
    31	    err.isPlannerError = true;
    32	    return err;
    33	  }
    34	
    35	  // --- fetchJson ---------------------------------------------------------------
    36	
    37	  function fetchJson(path, options) {
    38	    options = options || {};
    39	    var init = { method: options.method || "GET", headers: {} };
    40	    if (options.body !== undefined && options.body !== null) {
    41	      init.body = JSON.stringify(options.body);
    42	      init.headers["Content-Type"] = "application/json";
    43	    }
    44	    return fetch(path, init).then(
    45	      function (response) {
    46	        return response.text().then(function (raw) {
    47	          return { response: response, raw: raw };
    48	        });
    49	      },
    50	      function () {
    51	        return Promise.reject(makeError("network", "network error"));
    52	      }
    53	    ).then(function (pair) {
    54	      var response = pair.response;
    55	      var raw = pair.raw;
    56	      if (response.ok) {
    57	        try {
    58	          return raw === "" ? {} : JSON.parse(raw);
    59	        } catch (parseErr) {
    60	          return Promise.reject(
    61	            makeError("bad_json", "invalid JSON in response", { status: response.status })
    62	          );
    63	        }
    64	      }
    65	      var parsed = null;
    66	      try {
    67	        parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
    68	      } catch (parseErr) {
    69	        parsed = null;
    70	      }
    71	      if (parsed && parsed.error && parsed.error.code) {
    72	        return Promise.reject(makePlannerError(parsed.error, response.status));
    73	      }
    74	      return Promise.reject(
    75	        makeError("http_error", "HTTP " + response.status, { status: response.status })
    76	      );
    77	    });
    78	  }
    79	
    80	  Planner.api = { fetchJson: fetchJson };
    81	
    82	  // --- the invalidation bus ----------------------------------------------------
    83	
    84	  var cursor = 0;
    85	  var socket = null;
    86	  var debounceMs = config.DEBOUNCE_MS_DEFAULT;
    87	  var flushTimer = null;
    88	  var retryMs = config.WS_RETRY_MIN_MS;
    89	  var onFlush = null;
    90	  var started = false;
    91	
    92	  function flush() {
    93	    flushTimer = null;
    94	    window.__plannerDebug.flushes += 1;
    95	    console.debug("[planner] flush " + window.__plannerDebug.flushes);
    96	    if (onFlush) {
    97	      onFlush();
    98	    }
    99	  }
   100	
   101	  function schedule() {
   102	    clearTimeout(flushTimer);
   103	    flushTimer = setTimeout(flush, debounceMs);
   104	  }
   105	
   106	  function connect() {
   107	    var scheme = location.protocol === "https:" ? "wss://" : "ws://";
   108	    var url = scheme + location.host + config.API.EVENTS_WS + "?since=" + cursor;
   109	    socket = new WebSocket(url);
   110	    socket.onopen = function () {
   111	      retryMs = config.WS_RETRY_MIN_MS;
   112	      window.__plannerDebug.wsOpens += 1;
   113	      console.debug("[planner] ws open since=" + cursor);
   114	    };
   115	    socket.onmessage = function (event) {
   116	      var msg;
   117	      try {
   118	        msg = JSON.parse(event.data);
   119	      } catch (parseErr) {
   120	        console.debug("[planner] ws bad json");
   121	        return;
   122	      }
   123	      if (typeof msg.cursor === "number") {
   124	        cursor = msg.cursor;
   125	        window.__plannerDebug.cursor = cursor;
   126	      }
   127	      schedule();
   128	    };
   129	    socket.onclose = function () {
   130	      socket = null;
   131	      setTimeout(connect, retryMs);
   132	      retryMs = Math.min(retryMs * config.WS_RETRY_FACTOR, config.WS_RETRY_MAX_MS);
   133	    };
   134	  }
   135	
   136	  Planner.bus = {
   137	    onInvalidate: function (fn) {
   138	      onFlush = fn;
   139	    },
   140	    start: function (ms) {
   141	      if (started) {
   142	        return;
   143	      }
   144	      started = true;
   145	      debounceMs = ms;
   146	      connect();
   147	    }
   148	  };
   149	})();

     1	/* Minimal safe markdown renderer. The ONLY innerHTML assignment in the whole
     2	 * codebase lives here — and it receives a string whose every character of user
     3	 * origin was HTML-escaped BEFORE any parsing, so no un-entified "<", ">", or quote
     4	 * can exist. Every tag is renderer-authored from a closed set; the only attribute
     5	 * emitted is a scheme-whitelisted href. Classic script: attaches Planner.markdown. */
     6	(function () {
     7	  "use strict";
     8	  var Planner = (window.Planner = window.Planner || {});
     9	
    10	  // Code-span placeholder sentinel (NUL). Stripped from raw input first, so a
    11	  // user string can never forge a placeholder.
    12	  var NUL = String.fromCharCode(0);
    13	  var RESTORE_RE = new RegExp(NUL + "(\\d+)" + NUL, "g");
    14	
    15	  function escapeHtml(s) {
    16	    return s
    17	      .replace(/&/g, "&amp;")
    18	      .replace(/</g, "&lt;")
    19	      .replace(/>/g, "&gt;")
    20	      .replace(/"/g, "&quot;")
    21	      .replace(/'/g, "&#39;");
    22	  }
    23	
    24	  var SCHEME_RE = /^[a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*:/;
    25	
    26	  function safeHref(url) {
    27	    var lower = url.toLowerCase();
    28	    if (SCHEME_RE.test(lower)) {
    29	      return (
    30	        lower.indexOf("http:") === 0 ||
    31	        lower.indexOf("https:") === 0 ||
    32	        lower.indexOf("mailto:") === 0
    33	      );
    34	    }
    35	    return true; // scheme-less (relative, "#/ticket/...", "?", "/") is allowed
    36	  }
    37	
    38	  function renderInline(line) {
    39	    var codes = [];
    40	    // 1. Code spans first — protected from all further processing.
    41	    line = line.replace(/`([^`]+)`/g, function (match, inner) {
    42	      var index = codes.length;
    43	      codes.push(inner);
    44	      return NUL + index + NUL;
    45	    });
    46	    // 2. Links — href is scheme-whitelisted; the value is already escaped.
    47	    line = line.replace(/\[([^\]]+)\]\(([^()\s]+)\)/g, function (match, text, url) {
    48	      if (safeHref(url)) {
    49	        return '<a href="' + url + '">' + text + "</a>";
    50	      }
    51	      return match;
    52	    });
    53	    // 3. Bold before italic so "**" is not eaten by "*".
    54	    line = line.replace(/\*\*([^*]+)\*\*/g, "<strong>$1</strong>");
    55	    // 4. Italic.
    56	    line = line.replace(/\*([^*]+)\*/g, "<em>$1</em>");
    57	    line = line.replace(/_([^_]+)_/g, "<em>$1</em>");
    58	    // 5. Restore code spans.
    59	    line = line.replace(RESTORE_RE, function (match, index) {
    60	      return "<code>" + codes[Number(index)] + "</code>";
    61	    });
    62	    return line;
    63	  }
    64	
    65	  function render(text) {
    66	    // 1. Strip the placeholder sentinel so collisions are impossible.
    67	    text = String(text).split(NUL).join("");
    68	    // 2. Escape everything once, before any parsing.
    69	    var escaped = escapeHtml(text);
    70	    var lines = escaped.split("\n");
    71	    var out = [];
    72	    var para = null;
    73	    var listType = null;
    74	    var listItems = null;
    75	    var i = 0;
    76	
    77	    function closePara() {
    78	      if (para) {
    79	        out.push("<p>" + para.join("<br>") + "</p>");
    80	        para = null;
    81	      }
    82	    }
    83	    function closeList() {
    84	      if (listItems) {
    85	        out.push("<" + listType + ">" + listItems.join("") + "</" + listType + ">");
    86	        listItems = null;
    87	        listType = null;
    88	      }
    89	    }
    90	    function closeBlocks() {
    91	      closePara();
    92	      closeList();
    93	    }
    94	
    95	    while (i < lines.length) {
    96	      var line = lines[i];
    97	      // Fenced code block — verbatim, no inline processing (already escaped).
    98	      if (/^```/.test(line)) {
    99	        closeBlocks();
   100	        i += 1;
   101	        var codeLines = [];
   102	        while (i < lines.length && !/^```/.test(lines[i])) {
   103	          codeLines.push(lines[i]);
   104	          i += 1;
   105	        }
   106	        if (i < lines.length) {
   107	          i += 1; // consume the closing fence
   108	        }
   109	        out.push("<pre><code>" + codeLines.join("\n") + "</code></pre>");
   110	        continue;
   111	      }
   112	      // Blank line closes the open block.
   113	      if (/^\s*$/.test(line)) {
   114	        closeBlocks();
   115	        i += 1;
   116	        continue;
   117	      }
   118	      // Heading — caps at h3 (four or more "#" render as h3).
   119	      var heading = /^(#{1,6})\s+(.*)$/.exec(line);
   120	      if (heading) {
   121	        closeBlocks();
   122	        var level = heading[1].length;
   123	        if (level > 3) {
   124	          level = 3;
   125	        }
   126	        out.push("<h" + level + ">" + renderInline(heading[2]) + "</h" + level + ">");
   127	        i += 1;
   128	        continue;
   129	      }
   130	      // Unordered list item.
   131	      var unordered = /^[-*+]\s+(.*)$/.exec(line);
   132	      if (unordered) {
   133	        closePara();
   134	        if (listType && listType !== "ul") {
   135	          closeList();
   136	        }
   137	        if (!listItems) {
   138	          listType = "ul";
   139	          listItems = [];
   140	        }
   141	        listItems.push("<li>" + renderInline(unordered[1]) + "</li>");
   142	        i += 1;
   143	        continue;
   144	      }
   145	      // Ordered list item.
   146	      var ordered = /^\d{1,9}[.)]\s+(.*)$/.exec(line);
   147	      if (ordered) {
   148	        closePara();
   149	        if (listType && listType !== "ol") {
   150	          closeList();
   151	        }
   152	        if (!listItems) {
   153	          listType = "ol";
   154	          listItems = [];
   155	        }
   156	        listItems.push("<li>" + renderInline(ordered[1]) + "</li>");
   157	        i += 1;
   158	        continue;
   159	      }
   160	      // Paragraph line — consecutive lines join with <br>.
   161	      closeList();
   162	      if (!para) {
   163	        para = [];
   164	      }
   165	      para.push(renderInline(line));
   166	      i += 1;
   167	    }
   168	    closeBlocks();
   169	
   170	    var div = document.createElement("div");
   171	    div.className = "markdown";
   172	    div.innerHTML = out.join("");
   173	    return div;
   174	  }

   176	  Planner.markdown = { render: render };

     6	(function () {

    12	  var shell = null;

    16	    registry[name] = { render: render };
    17	  };

    19	  function quietLine(text) {
    20	    var div = document.createElement("div");
    21	    div.className = "quiet-line";
    22	    div.textContent = text;
    23	    return div;
    24	  }

    30	      root.appendChild(quietLine("not built yet"));
    31	    });
    32	  });
    33	
    34	  function route() {
    35	    var hash = location.hash;
    36	    if (hash === "" || hash === "#") {
    37	      location.replace(config.ROUTES.day);
    38	      return;
    39	    }
    40	    var segments = hash.slice(1).split("/").filter(Boolean);
    41	    var name = segments[0];
    42	    var params = {};
    43	    if (name === "ticket") {
    44	      params.id = segments[1];
    45	    }
    46	    var known = Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(registry, name);
    47	    var badShape = name === "ticket" && !params.id;
    48	    if (!known || badShape) {
    49	      shell.setActiveNav(null);
    50	      shell.content.replaceChildren(quietLine("no such screen"));
    51	      return;
    52	    }
    53	    shell.setActiveNav(name);
    54	    var entrance = hash !== lastHash;
    55	    lastHash = hash;
    56	    var screenDiv = document.createElement("div");
    57	    screenDiv.className = entrance ? "screen screen-enter" : "screen";
    58	    shell.content.replaceChildren(screenDiv);
    59	    registry[name].render(screenDiv, params);
    60	  }

    63	    Planner.api.fetchJson(config.API.QUEUES).then(
    64	      function (res) {
    65	        shell.setReviewBadge((res.approvals || []).length);
    66	      },

    68	        console.debug("[planner] queues fetch failed; badge unchanged");
    69	      }

    71	  }

    61	def free_or(preferred: int) -> int:
    62	    with socket.socket(socket.AF_INET, socket.SOCK_STREAM) as probe:
    63	        try:
    64	            probe.bind(("127.0.0.1", preferred))
    65	            return preferred
    66	        except OSError:
    67	            pass
    68	    with socket.socket(socket.AF_INET, socket.SOCK_STREAM) as fallback:
    69	        fallback.bind(("127.0.0.1", 0))
    70	        return int(fallback.getsockname()[1])
    71

    73	def wait_ready(base: str, budget: float = 15.0, interval: float = 0.25) -> None:
    74	    deadline = time.time() + budget
    75	    while time.time() < deadline:
    76	        try:
    77	            if httpx.get(f"{base}/api/meta", timeout=1.0).status_code == 200:
    78	                return
    79	        except httpx.HTTPError:
    80	            pass
    81	        time.sleep(interval)
    82	    raise AssertionError("server did not become ready within budget")
    83

"""FastAPI app factory shell. Mounts the domain routers under /api, serves the
static assets and the app shell, exposes GET /api/meta and the event WebSocket,
and — only in test mode — the /api/test/* control endpoints. The resolution
engine, dispatcher, and real route bodies land in later stages; here the surface
and the wiring are fixed.

This module and the domain api.py files are the only places FastAPI/pydantic
appear."""

from __future__ import annotations

import importlib
import logging
import sqlite3
from collections.abc import AsyncIterator, Callable
from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

from fastapi import FastAPI, Request, WebSocket
from fastapi.responses import HTMLResponse, JSONResponse
from fastapi.staticfiles import StaticFiles

from planner.chat.api import router as chat_router
from planner.core.adapters.registry import Adapters
from planner.core.clock import Clock
from planner.core.config import Config
from planner.core.errors import ErrorCode, PlannerError
from planner.core.testmode import build_test_router
from planner.core.ws import tail_events
from planner.days.api import router as days_router
from planner.dispatch.api import router as dispatch_router
from planner.seed.api import router as seed_router
from planner.sprints.api import router as sprints_router
from planner.tickets.api import router as tickets_router

_log = logging.getLogger("planner.server")

_STATUS_BY_CODE: dict[ErrorCode, int] = {
    ErrorCode.not_found: 404,
    ErrorCode.stale_claim: 409,
    ErrorCode.gateway_offline: 503,
}

_SHELL = "<!doctype html><meta charset=utf-8><title>planner</title><div id=app></div>"


def http_status_for(code: ErrorCode) -> int:
    return _STATUS_BY_CODE.get(code, 400)


def create_app(
    config: Config,
    clock: Clock,
    adapters: Adapters,
    conn_factory: Callable[[], sqlite3.Connection],
) -> FastAPI:
    # The DDL carries the literal 200 title cap; the config value is what write
    # paths enforce. Guard that they agree (amendment 11). Factory time is startup
    # and strictly earlier than any request, so this fires even for TestClient apps
    # that never enter the lifespan.
    assert config.title_max_chars == 200, "title_max_chars must equal the DDL literal (200)"

    @asynccontextmanager
    async def _lifespan(app_: FastAPI) -> AsyncIterator[None]:
        Path(config.db_path).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        Path(config.logs_dir).mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        loops: Any = None
        if not config.test_mode:  # D6: background loops never run in test mode
            try:
                module = importlib.import_module("planner.core.loops")
                start = module.start_background_loops
            except (ImportError, AttributeError):
                _log.warning("planner.core.loops unavailable; running without background loops")
            else:
                loops = start(config, clock, adapters, conn_factory)
        try:
            yield
        finally:
            if loops is not None:
                await loops.stop()

    app = FastAPI(title="planner", version="2.0.0", lifespan=_lifespan)
    app.state.config = config
    app.state.clock = clock
    app.state.adapters = adapters
    app.state.conn_factory = conn_factory

    @app.exception_handler(PlannerError)
    async def handle_planner_error(request: Request, exc: PlannerError) -> JSONResponse:
        return JSONResponse(status_code=http_status_for(exc.code), content=exc.to_payload())

    for domain_router in (
        tickets_router,
        sprints_router,
        days_router,
        dispatch_router,
        seed_router,
        chat_router,
    ):
        app.include_router(domain_router, prefix="/api")

    @app.get("/api/meta")
    async def meta() -> dict[str, Any]:
        return {
            "ui_debounce_ms": config.ui_debounce_ms,
            "ws_poll_ms": config.ws_poll_ms,
            "test_mode": config.test_mode,
        }

    @app.websocket("/api/events")
    async def events_ws(websocket: WebSocket, since: int = 0) -> None:
        await tail_events(
            websocket, since, conn_factory, config.ws_poll_ms, config.events_read_limit
        )

    @app.get("/", response_class=HTMLResponse)
    async def index() -> str:
        return _SHELL

    if config.test_mode:
        app.include_router(
            build_test_router(config, clock, adapters, conn_factory), prefix="/api"
        )

    app.mount("/assets", StaticFiles(directory="assets"), name="assets")
    return app

The team lead directed the pipeline to proceed: the orchestrator's sense-check (below) was written and the implementer dispatched while the stdin-fixed codex review runs in parallel; codex findings are dispositioned in this file when they land and any real ones are folded in before/with the diff review. orchestration/tickets/T14-ui-foundation/impl-review-raw.txt:648:     6	**Approach.** Five classic scripts compose under one namespace object `window.Planner` (each file is an IIFE that does `window.Planner = window.Planner || {}` and attaches exactly one sub-namespace), so seven asset files load in `<script src>` order with no bundler and every file passes `node --check`. The data layer is deliberately stateless: `api.js` holds only a WS cursor, a debounce timer, a backoff counter, and one registered flush callback; every flush makes `app.js` re-run the active screen's `render(root, params)`, which refetches full JSON — WS payloads are never read beyond `msg.cursor` (SPEC §9 audit target). Rendering primitives are the seven D11 components, all styled in `app.css` exclusively through `var(--token)`; the whole personality (restrained neutral-dark surfaces, one amber accent meaning "needs the human", five type sizes, ease-out motion) lives in `tokens.css`. The server change is confined to the `_SHELL` string. The smoke gate boots in-process uvicorn in test mode and drives real headless chromium to prove: shell references in order, meta keys, default route + placeholders, and the event-append → one-debounced-flush invariant (single event ⇒ exactly 1 flush; two rapid events ⇒ exactly 1 flush).

orchestration/tickets/T14-ui-foundation/plan-review-raw.txt:379:   228	- **Contracts first**: each domain has a contracts file (types, enums, shapes) generated at stage 1; all implementation imports its types from contracts and never redeclares a shape locally; the frontend consumes exactly the JSON shapes the contracts document. A shape change means changing the contract file and letting type errors drive the fixes.
orchestration/tickets/T14-ui-foundation/plan-review-raw.txt:381:   230	- **Audio capture seam (future, reserved).** Audio capture is not in v1, but its arrival point is fixed now: it will be its own isolated domain module behind an adapter interface, feeding the chat input. Build the chat input as a component that accepts pluggable input sources, and couple nothing to the assumption that input is typed text — dropping the audio module in later must touch its own new folder and one registration, nothing else.
orchestration/tickets/T14-ui-foundation/plan-review-raw.txt:400:   249	**Build in this order**: (1) contracts skeleton — schema DDL, enums, typed models, adapter interfaces, config loading, CLI/API surface stubs; (2) **the verify instrument** (18.2), runnable with every checklist item FAIL; (3) pure-logic modules + unit tests (items 1–21 and 36) green; (4) server wiring — API, WS, resolution engine over DB, dispatcher, boundary scheduler, CLI against the API; (5) UI views; (6) Playwright e2e suite, items 22–34 green; (7) the fake-ticket dogfood pass (Section 18.5): Level A green (item 35), Levels B and C executed with evidence recorded in DOGFOOD.md. Do not start a later stage while an earlier stage's tests fail.
orchestration/tickets/T14-ui-foundation/plan-review-raw.txt:488:     6	**Approach.** Five classic scripts compose under one namespace object `window.Planner` (each file is an IIFE that does `window.Planner = window.Planner || {}` and attaches exactly one sub-namespace), so seven asset files load in `<script src>` order with no bundler and every file passes `node --check`. The data layer is deliberately stateless: `api.js` holds only a WS cursor, a debounce timer, a backoff counter, and one registered flush callback; every flush makes `app.js` re-run the active screen's `render(root, params)`, which refetches full JSON — WS payloads are never read beyond `msg.cursor` (SPEC §9 audit target). Rendering primitives are the seven D11 components, all styled in `app.css` exclusively through `var(--token)`; the whole personality (restrained neutral-dark surfaces, one amber accent meaning "needs the human", five type sizes, ease-out motion) lives in `tokens.css`. The server change is confined to the `_SHELL` string. The smoke gate boots in-process uvicorn in test mode and drives real headless chromium to prove: shell references in order, meta keys, default route + placeholders, and the event-append → one-debounced-flush invariant (single event ⇒ exactly 1 flush; two rapid events ⇒ exactly 1 flush).

orchestration/tickets/T14-ui-foundation/plan-review-raw.txt:2267:orchestration/tickets/T14-ui-foundation/plan.md:6:**Approach.** Five classic scripts compose under one namespace object `window.Planner` (each file is an IIFE that does `window.Planner = window.Planner || {}` and attaches exactly one sub-namespace), so seven asset files load in `<script src>` order with no bundler and every file passes `node --check`. The data layer is deliberately stateless: `api.js` holds only a WS cursor, a debounce timer, a backoff counter, and one registered flush callback; every flush makes `app.js` re-run the active screen's `render(root, params)`, which refetches full JSON — WS payloads are never read beyond `msg.cursor` (SPEC §9 audit target). The server change is confined to the `_SHELL` string. The smoke gate boots in-process uvicorn in test mode and drives real headless chromium to prove: shell references in order, meta keys, default route + placeholders, and the event-append → one-debounced-flush invariant (single event ⇒ exactly 1 flush; two rapid events ⇒ exactly 1 flush).

orchestration/tickets/T14-ui-foundation/plan-review-raw.txt:2822:orchestration/tickets/T14-ui-foundation/plan.md:6:**Approach.** Five classic scripts compose under one namespace object `window.Planner` (each file is an IIFE that does `window.Planner = window.Planner || {}` and attaches exactly one sub-namespace), so seven asset files load in `<script src>` order with no bundler and every file passes `node --check`. The data layer is deliberately stateless: `api.js` holds only a WS cursor, a debounce timer, a backoff counter, and one registered flush callback; every flush makes `app.js` re-run the active screen's `render(root, params)`, which refetches full JSON — WS payloads are never read beyond `msg.cursor` (SPEC §9 audit target). Rendering primitives are the seven D11 components, all styled in `app.css` exclusively through `var(--token)`; the whole personality (restrained neutral-dark surfaces, one amber accent meaning "needs the human", five type sizes, ease-out motion) lives in `tokens.css`. The server change is confined to the `_SHELL` string. The smoke gate boots in-process uvicorn in test mode and drives real headless chromium to prove: shell references in order, meta keys, default route + placeholders, and the event-append → one-debounced-flush invariant (single event ⇒ exactly 1 flush; two rapid events ⇒ exactly 1 flush).

codex
**Findings**
- HIGH — [assets/markdown.js](/Users/khushaljagota/.hermes/planning-v2/assets/markdown.js:26): `safeHref()` checks the pre-normalized URL. A literal leading C0 control char, e.g. `[x](\u0001javascript:alert%281%29)`, survives escaping, is treated as scheme-less, then browser URL parsing normalizes it to `javascript:`. Fix: reject C0 controls before allowing hrefs, and validate the normalized value before emitting it.
- MEDIUM — [smoke.py](/Users/khushaljagota/.hermes/planning-v2/orchestration/tickets/T14-ui-foundation/smoke.py:165): the flush assertions watch `window.__plannerDebug.flushes`, which increments inside the bus before and independent of `onFlush`. The smoke would pass even if `app.js` never registered the invalidation callback. Fix: instrument a registered screen render/callback counter and assert it increments exactly once per append.
- MINOR — [assets/app.js](/Users/khushaljagota/.hermes/planning-v2/assets/app.js:46): route shape validation accepts extras like `#/day/junk` and `#/ticket/t_x/junk`; T14 only defines exact route shapes. Fix: reject `segments.length !== 1` for normal screens and `segments.length !== 2 || !id` for tickets.

**Clean Categories**
- Server diff: clean. `src/planner/core/server.py` diff only changes `_SHELL`; assert, lifespan, handler, routers, meta, WS, test router, and `StaticFiles` mount are untouched.
- `innerHTML`: clean. Only real assignment is [assets/markdown.js](/Users/khushaljagota/.hermes/planning-v2/assets/markdown.js:172).
- Markdown escape/code sentinel: clean apart from the href finding. Escape happens before parsing; raw NUL is stripped before sentinels are introduced.
- Client state store: clean. No entity JSON cache, no `msg.events` reads; app state is route/registry/shell only.
- `app.css` literals: clean. Grep found no literal colors or `px/rem/em/ms/s` outside tokens.
- JS syntax fence: clean. No `import`/`export`/`await`; `node --check` passed for all five asset JS files.
- Components: clean. Exports only D11 items 1-6 and 17; five-link nav and DOM-node `appShell` are logged deviations.
- SPEC §14/15: clean. No chat/audio coupling, command palette, keyboard layer, drag/drop, instructional empty states, or non-goal widgets.

I did not run the full smoke: the read-only sandbox blocked Playwright browser launch with a temp-dir `EPERM`.
